## Units

State is one of `not started`, `stand-in`, `in progress` or `finished`. A finished unit names the commit it was finished on and what ended its loop: `passed`, `round limit` or `clock`. A unit is never marked finished because of a budget report. It stays `in progress`.

## Tool record

What this workspace can call, measured in Phase 0. It includes the exact method that displays a picture inline in the task thread.

### Inline display method (proven 2026-11-21 01:00 UTC with `references/approved/contact-sheet.jpg`)

1. `npm run status` writes `out/work/status/status.json`: `{"pics":[{"label","w","h","src"}],"lines":[...]}`. Each picture is a JPEG of at most `status.pictureLongEdgePx` on the long edge, stored as a base64 data URL. `npm run inline -- --name X "caption=file"` writes the same kind of file for any other picture.
2. The Builder calls the inline visualization tool with `sources = {"pics": {"workspace_path": "<absolute path of that json>", "format": "json"}}` and the widget code kept in `tools/status-widget.html`, word for word.
3. The widget reads `window.visualization.sources.pics.data`, writes the status lines above the pictures and sets each `img.src` from the data URL in a script placed after the markup. One picture fills the width. Two to four form a grid with a caption under each.
- What does not work in the widget: external URLs and workspace paths as `img.src` (blocked by its content policy). A file attachment alone does not display inline.
- The widget is about 720 px wide and its visible height is capped near 640 px, so four 16:9 pictures in a 2 by 2 grid fit.
- Fallback if the tool ever fails: attach the pictures as files at the same rhythm and publish with every status.

### What this workspace can call (recorded 2026-11-21 01:14 UTC)

- Image generation. Models: `seedream_5` (up to 10 reference images, about 2K, the one the reference run found best for this look), `nano_banana_pro` (14 references, 2K), `nano_banana_2_1` (14 references), `gpt_image_2_6_flare` and `gpt_image_2_6_sunburst` (5 references, transparent backgrounds, they ignore the aspect ratio setting). Aspect ratios 1:1, 3:4, 4:3, 9:16, 16:9. The largest result seen in the reference run was about 2816 x 1584.
- Still to video generation exists. Not used so far.
- Image search and image fetch exist.
- App hosting: deploy a static folder, then publish it at a slug. An earlier build measured about 500 MB per site. Organization-only sites ask for a sign-in, so the harness cannot read them back.
- Publishing, as done on 2026-11-21 01:56 UTC: `npm run review`, then deploy the folder `dist/` under the site name `Sunflower`, then publish it. The first publish used the address `sunflower-film` (`slug` in the config). Every later publish updates site id `547457d5-7066-4871-b762-1715efa50d06` (app id `21dfc1c9-6bc5-4694-8885-f131c248f57f`). The site's visibility is Organization, so it asks for a sign-in and the harness tests the same `dist/` served locally.
- Inline visualization: the method above.
- A text-only model tool that cannot see images, so every critic is a spawned agent.
- Supporting agents, in this VM or in their own sandbox VM. At most `sandbox.maxAgents` at once.

### Model families for supporting agents (measured 2026-11-21 01:01 to 01:06 UTC)

Each family was sent the same three numbered test charts (1400 px and 2000 px) and asked to read the marks. All six read every row they could resolve correctly, with no wrong numbers.

| Family id | Sees images | Note |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `gpt_6_2_sol` | yes | read every row |
| `gemini_4_carbon` | yes | read every row |
| `claude_opus_5_5` | yes | read every row |
| `pplx_computer_glm_5_3_1009` | yes | read every row |
| `grok_4_7` | yes | read every row |
| `kimi_k3` | yes | could not read the smallest (7 px) row at any size. The weakest for fine detail |

Other ids that exist and were not tested: `gpt_6_2_astra`, `gemini_neon`, `claude_fable_5_2`, `claude_opus_5_5_fast`, `claude_sonnet_5_5`. `deepseek_v4_pro_0813` is not available.

## Render budget

Seconds per sample and per frame at each format and tier, the time for one judged still at the `final` tier, the time for the whole film at each tier, and the texture memory. Measured in Phases 0, 2, 3 and 5.

### Phase 3 look lock (real plant, golden world, real field; five worlds still stand-ins)

The run took 19 min 25 s and ended with exit 0. Its files are in `out/work/budget/phase3/`. One sample is one full render of the scene. ## Look lock

Written at the end of Phase 3: every locked setting, each technique that worked and each that did not.

Written 2026-11-21 at the end of Phase 3. The build it describes is `cd262c1` (engine `5ac2814e4342d561`), the one round 6 judged. The bloom's loop ended at its limit (four rounds, 3 to 6), not at a pass: round 6 scored R1 5, R3 4 and R4 5 where 9 is needed from each, the jury passed with 7 of 10 against the controls' 6 of 10, and the machine checks and M1 to M3 were green. What is still open is under "Open items".

### Locked settings

Camera and lens of the bloom (`config/showcase.json`, `src/units/bloom/index.ts`, `src/director/pose.ts`, `src/core/sampler.ts`):

- 35 mm on a 36 mm gate. The keys at 45.0 s and 50.5 s are fitted to the approved images and stay as they are: distance 0.7878 and 0.8685 m, height 1.6016 and 1.6595 m, aim height 1.5881 and 1.6445 m, `frameX` 0.6482 and 0.6115, azimuth 17.88 and 20.0 degrees.
- Seven keys between them (45.25, 45.5, 46.5, 47.5, 48.5, 49.5, 50.25 s) hold the view of the far background within 0.1 px while distance, height and azimuth run evenly. The key at 52 s takes 0.6 of its old step, so the crane starts gently. This is the judged build's path and not a locked one: round 6's motion critic read the hold as a stop (see "What did not work"), and Phase 5 replaces the hold with a slow continuous move through the two fitted keys.
- f/4 through the whole bloom (`fNumber: { keys: [4, 4] }`). At f/5.6 the heads behind the plant showed their separate rays; at f/2 the bracts at the rim of the bud were soft and the field's heads were blank discs.
- One focus distance for the frame, shared between the head's held point and the stem 0.2 m under its end; the held point takes at most `HELD_SHARE` 0.55 of `detail.focusCocPx`. Nothing is sharpened or blurred per object.
- The lens samples of every frame are centered on the axis (two passes that subtract the weighted middle). Without it everything out of focus shifted by up to 2.5 px from frame to frame at 8 samples.
- Tiers: `draft` 1, `review` 8, `final` 48 samples a frame.

The golden world (`src/worlds/golden/index.ts`, `layers.ts`, `sky.ts`, `air.ts`, `src/weather/skyline.ts`):

- Sun: azimuth -133.67 degrees, elevation 2.18 degrees, color 8.5, 5.0, 2.0, softness 1.2 degrees, `lean` 0.8, `lift` 1.
- Sky: zenith 0.055, 0.075, 0.115, horizon 0.26, 0.2, 0.135, bounce 0.04, 0.028, 0.014, `ambient` 3, glow 1, 0.62, 0.27 with power 40, disc 90, sky gain 1.4. The sky is computed, with no backdrop picture.
- Fog: color 0.02, 0.026, 0.022, sun tint 1.6, 0.95, 0.45, density 0.003, falloff 0.5. Wind 0.5.
- The land: the field ends 66 m out on a straight edge, two rows of real trees stand at 79 and 84 m, and nine layers of one shared skyline run from 68 m to 5900 m. Every world draws that same skyline in its own colors, so the horizon holds across a cut.
- Nine banks of shaded evening air over the far field (`SHADES`), three broad broken shafts from the sun, motes in the air.

Grade and lens look of the golden world (the film's defaults for a sunlit world; `src/worlds/golden/index.ts`, `src/post/looks.ts`):

- Grade: exposure 0.74, contrast 1.3, white 9, saturation 1.06, gain 1.03, 1, 0.96, lift 0.0016, 0.005, 0.0042, bloom 0.13 with knee 1.1, vignette 0.3, grain 0.012. `grade.lift` is the Builder's.
- Lens look: veil 1.6 tinted 1.15, 0.62, 0.3, flare 0, halation 0.5, core 3.2, star 0, glow 0.065, shafts 0.008, rays 1.2, `black` 1, 0.55, 1 (the toe that takes the lift back in the deepest shade only), `specks` 1 (lone lights out of focus are not held down). `black` and `specks` are set per world.

The plant (`config/showcase.json`, `src/plant/growth.ts`, `src/plant/standin.ts`):

- Fixed facts: full height 1.85 m, head 0.30 m across, disc 0.16 m, 24 leaves, 34 ray florets, 1500 disc florets on the golden angle.
- The head's facing, the stem's radius (it loses 0.28 of its radius from foot to top), `SEAT` 0.86, `NECK_FLARE` 0.44, `BRACT_LONGEST` 0.085 m, `RAY_SHUT` 0.85, `RAY_FIRST` 2.8, `NEIGHBOR_HEAD` 0.7. The node of the leaf under the head stays at about 1.477 m while the stem above it lengthens.
- 63 broad pointed bracts on the hero's bud, 54000 hairs on the leaves, stem hairs 0.14 mm wide.

The field (`config/showcase.json`, `src/field/`):

- A plant every 0.45 m in rows 0.75 m apart. Neighbors lead the plant into bloom, they do not lag it.
- The companion: `leftM` 0.722, `towardCameraM` 0.08, `heightRatio` 1.131, its leaves turned and held by hand (`setLeafTurn`, `setLeafHold`), its sun floor 0.6.

### What worked

- Fitting the camera to the approved image first, then building the part until it sits where the approved has it. Every dispute about a part's size or place was settled by measuring both images in pixels at 1920.
- Measuring tones in boxes on the approved pair (tenth, middle and ninetieth percentile) and giving each builder the numbers for its part. "Too bright" never moved anything; "33,37,18 against 20,31,22 in this box" did.
- Filling the golden hour from the sky (`sky.ambient` 0.8 to 3) to get green into the shaded head and bracts, with a toe in the lens to take the black back.
- Real things at the right scale as the source of sharpness. M3 rose from 0.755 and 0.825 to 0.96 and 1.02 at `review` when the leaves got their detail map at true size, veins and hairs at the rim, the bracts their points and ribs, and the disc separate buds. Grain and post sharpening were never used to pass it.
- Lit hairs on the stem and the bud's rim. Four of the seven jurors who said "top-budget film" in round 5 gave the hairs as their reason. Hairs shorter than 2 px on screen do not show; they have to be long and wide enough at 1920.
- The disc as separate florets and buds on the spiral with a dark gap round each. Strands and a painted swirl in the middle read as generated art to two jurors.
- Cupped rays that stand on the rim with uneven veins as ribs. Evenly spaced grooves read as molded ribbons.
- Broad broken shafts and a warm halo for the sun instead of a hard point with a star and ruled spokes, which critics named in three rounds. The size is not settled: round 6's wide white core (`core` 3.2) was called too large by its match critic ("a large round white bloom and broad yellow wash swallow the leaf edge and nearby horizon"), so the right sun lies between round 5's and round 6's: a small hot spot just under the leaf, directional rays, the sky and the ridge still readable round it.
- One shared skyline for all worlds, drawn by each in its own colors. It brought the horizon's step across the cut at 40 s from about 34 px to 4 px.
- A second worktree for integration: branches are merged and measured there while the main tree renders a round, and `main` only moves by fast forward.
- The clip rendered in twelve parts on the builders' machines and joined by its hashes.

### What did not work

- Star spikes and thin rays on the sun, at any strength.
- Light through the whole face of a leaf: it made the leaves glow a flat yellow green. The approved's leaves are dark blue green with a thin lit rim and a few patches.
- Quieting the rays' veins and fading the bracts' relief to remove ruled lines and blocks: both cost M3 (0.722 to 0.672 and 0.849 to 0.786 at `final`) without replacing the detail. The detail has to change kind, not just fall away.
- A comb of narrow straps for the cap of shut rays; inner bracts lying over the face of the shut bud (a vertex moved 40.9 mm in one frame).
- A brighter gain on the hairs against the sun (1.8 to 4.2): M3 passed with it, but the stills showed white beaded rails along the stem, the leaf margins and the bracts. A sharpness reading bought with specks is not kept; twice as many strands half as bright took its place.
- A floor on the far side fill of the lens: it changed the glare with the tier.
- The field's sun shafts into the crop's tops for the bloom: the left middle was already brighter than the approved. It is kept on its branch for the `field` unit.
- Reading M3 at `draft`. It reads higher than `review`, and `review` reads about 0.08 (frame 1350) and 0.04 (frame 1515) higher than `final`. M3 is quoted at `review` or `final` only.
- One glare strength for both keys: tuned at 50.5 s it is too bright at 45 s.
- Either way of joining the two bloom keys so far. Channels that each ran their own curve between them made the far background drift 11.5 px and come back (round 5: "stops and reverses direction"). Holding one view of the far background between them took the reversal out and put a hold in (round 6: "the camera stops ... holds at 106 px for about 7.5 s"). The two approved images share one view of the far background, so the move between them has to be made on purpose: a slow continuous move that never settles, which Phase 5's smoothing pass sets.
- Frame to frame sameness of small parts was not looked at until the last round: a ray that appears between two frames (1339 to 1340) and inner petals that re-form from frame to frame were named by round 6's motion critic. Every part that opens needs its own continuous path, checked on consecutive frames at native size.

### What the other five units start from

- The plant, the surface shading, the hairs, the field and the lens are shared code; a world sets light, sky, air, the state of the ground and its weather, and its own `black` and `specks`.
- A world draws the shared skyline at its true height, however little of it its air lets through.
- Every builder measures its part against the approved image in boxes, at `review`, and reports numbers with pictures at native size.
- The picture must be the same twice: no derivative after a discard, no size that is set after it is used, nothing that depends on the order of frames. `npm run determinism` before every report.
- Parts placed by the weather read the frame's state before the plant prepares (`src/director/film.ts`).

## Decisions made by the Builder

One line each: the date, the phase, the decision and why.

- 2026-11-21 · Phase 0 · The clock was started at the task start (00:51:51 UTC) with `--start --at`, not at the minute the script first ran, so the reading time before the clock existed is counted as working time.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 0 · Harness scripts are TypeScript run with `tsx`, so they import `src/config.ts` and the timeline directly and no number is typed twice.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 0 · `.npmrc` sets `legacy-peer-deps=true`: `npm install` crashed inside its peer dependency resolver (`Cannot read properties of null (reading 'edgesOut')`) on the current test runner's peer list. With the flag the same pinned versions install cleanly.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 0 · Added two helper commands that the kit does not list: `npm run push` (fetch, merge, commit with the trailer, push, log the push) and `npm run inline` (pack any picture for the inline display).
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 0 · MP4 muxing for Export uses `mp4-muxer` pinned at its last release. Its author now points to a larger successor library. The small one does exactly what Export needs and its interface is known, so it is the lower risk.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 0 · Render path: three.js `WebGLRenderer` on WebGL2 with `antialias: false`, pixel ratio 1 and custom GLSL3 materials that share one set of uniforms. Each sample is rendered to an RGBA16F target with depth and added into an RGBA32F ping-pong accumulator. Lens and grade run once after accumulation. One pixel is read back before any timer stops.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 0 · Motion inside the shutter: the plant is built on the CPU at shutter open and shutter close and the vertex shader mixes the two by the sample's shutter position. The camera is evaluated exactly at each sample's own time. The shutter opens at the frame's time and lasts `film.shutterDegrees / 360` of a frame.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 0 · The alpha channel of a sample carries the blur circle in pixels, so the accumulated frame knows how soft each pixel is. A blur guided by it fills the gaps between lens samples (radius = blur circle / sqrt(samples)), and the far backdrop is blurred analytically. Both are allowed by `kit/04-LOOK.md` section 7.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 0 · Camera convention: plant foot at the origin, y up, camera at `(d sin az, h, d cos az)`. `frameX` is reached by turning the lens only. The pan is solved so the point of the plant axis at `aimHeightM` lands exactly at `frameX` and at half height. No roll. Focus distance is the depth of the growing tip. The f-number is not in the config, so it lives in the units' camera fit.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 0 · G10: the page exposes one extra call, `requirements()`, that lists the WebGL2 extensions and limits the app needs. The gate compares it with `config/webgl-caps.json`.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 0 · The harness serves the built `dist/` from a local server on `localhost` and rebuilds when the hashes of the source differ from the build. Engine and unit hashes come from one shared library and are injected at build time.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 0 · Stand-in systems live in the real folders and register themselves with `declareStandIn`, so `info().standIns` is always the truth.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 0 · World settings live in `src/worlds/<id>/index.ts` and each unit's camera fit in `src/units/<id>/index.ts` (the f-number of each key, in the order of the config), so a unit's hash changes only when its own look changes. Shared sky and weather code lives in `src/weather/`, inside the engine hash.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 0 · `index.html` and `config/webgl-caps.json` are counted in the engine hash, because both change what a frame can be.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 0 · The sky and the far backdrop are drawn per sample from the pixel's central ray and blurred by formula from the blur circle at infinity (a wider sun disc, a lower mip of the backdrop). The stand-in backdrop of each world is a blurred copy of its approved image made by `tools/standin-backdrops.ts`, mirrored sideways so it has no edge. Phase 2 replaces it with the painted-out backdrop the build order asks for.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 0 · Lens and grade order, once per frame after accumulation: gap fill blur guided by the blur circle, bloom from a soft knee, exposure, color gain, vignette, a filmic curve with a toe and a shoulder, saturation, lift, grain, dither, sRGB.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 0 · The sampler was changed after its unit test showed that 8 samples could leave one side of the lens empty. The lens and the soft light now each use a rotated Hammersley set in their own shuffled order. The pixel keeps a rotated Halton set, the shutter one sample per slot.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 0 · `npm run determinism` renders three judged frames (the second, the middle and the second to last of the list) in the master format at the `review` tier on one fresh page, renders them in reverse order on a second fresh page, renders one of them twice on a third, and compares every pixel. `--frames`, `--tier` and `--format` change the set.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 0 · `npm run gate -- --phase N` runs these checks: Phase 0 G1, G2, G3, G4, G10. Phase 1 D1. Phase 2 G1 to G12, D2, D3. Phases 3 and 4 G1 to G13. Phase 5 G1 to G13 and D2. Phase 6 G1 to G13, D2, D3. `--machine` runs the machine checks alone, for the start of a round.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 0 · G8 reads a generated file as covered when `assets-src/<owner>/RECIPE.md` exists and names the file. The owner is `plant`, `ground` or `worlds/<id>`.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 0 · The review page keeps the whole site under 450 MB (the hosting took about 500 MB in an earlier build). If the videos do not fit, the largest are left out and the page says so.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 0 · The app was published at the address `sunflower-film` without asking first. The reasons: the build prompt orders the whole kit and says not to ask questions, `kit/05-ARCHITECTURE.md` says to publish `dist/` with the workspace's own app hosting at `slug` from the config, and the site's visibility is Organization, so only people signed in to the organization can open it.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 1 · The rough cut pushes each approved still by at most 10 percent about the point where the plant's stem stands in the frame (`frameX`, half height), in or out as the camera's focal length over distance changes across the segment. `dawn` and `bloom` have two keys each and dissolve from the first still to the second between the two key times on a smooth ramp. Between `bloom` and `field` the film has no cut, but the rough cut changes still there, because the two stills are different pictures.

- 2026-11-21 · Phase 2 · The page exposes two more calls for the measures: `check(frame)` (invalid values in the frame before lens and grade, where the growing tip lands and the blur circle there, for M1) and `texels(frame)` (texels per screen pixel over the textured pixels of the sharp zone, in the `texels` view at one sample, for M2).
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 2 · Found while testing the texel count: the ground had never been drawn. Its triangles faced down and were culled, so the soft backdrop showed where soil should be. The winding is fixed. Every Phase 0 spike frame was made without ground.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 2 · Ownership for the two build agents is by file where a folder is shared: `export_builder` owns `src/capture/export.ts`, its style sheet and `tools/proof.ts`. `harness_builder` owns the rest of the missing commands under `tools/`. The Builder keeps `src/` and the gate, clock, status, push, review and budget tools.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 2 · The reference board and the motion studies are gathered now by four scouts, as the build order allows, so the board is ready when Phase 3 opens. The Builder assembles `references/BOARD.md` from their notes.

- 2026-11-21 · Phase 2 · Backdrops. Each world's backdrop is its approved image with the plant painted out by an image model that was given that image as its only input: dawn from `dawn-seedling`, rain from `rain`, night from `night`, frost from `frost`, golden from `bloom-hero` (the companion's leaves painted out too, in a second step). Ten takes were made and one kept per world, two takes each and four for golden, of six allowed. A take was thrown out when anything but the plant had changed: a horizon that moved, a picture that shifted by 2 px, a recolored sky, grass or a plant that was added. The prompts and the checks are in each world's recipe.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 2 · The backdrop is locked to the camera of its key: its center sits where that key's lens points and its width is that key's field of view, so at the key's time the backdrop lies exactly where the approved image has it. Past its edges the picture fades into its own edge color, so no second sun appears when the camera turns. The ground and the field cover everything below the horizon.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 2 · The pose math moved to `src/director/pose.ts`, which does not load the registry, so harness scripts can project points without a browser. `film.view` holds the only changes the live mode may make to the picture: the camera turned about its aim point, its distance, and a world held at any time. With all of them at rest the film is unchanged.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 2 · The modes went to a third build agent (`modes_builder`) so the plant, the field and the camera fit are not held up by the interface. It owns the mode files and builds against `film.view`.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 2 · Field rows run at a right angle to the direction `field.companion.fromAzimuthDeg`, fixed in the ground, so the rows read as rows from the hero camera and the field key alike.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 2 · Blockout pack: the key pairs are tiled three to an image with halves labeled `N-a` (the approved image) and `N-b` (the frame), so the pack is three film sheets, four cut sheets and three pair images: ten files, one message.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 2 · Where a backdrop holds the sun, the light comes from where the sun sits in the picture (`backdrop.sunAt`), so shadows and rim light agree with the sky. The elevation read from the picture is held at 2° or more, so a sun on the horizon line still lights the plant. The drawn sun disc is switched off in those worlds (dawn, frost, golden), because the picture already has its sun.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 2 · Stand-in backdrops are drawn with `blur: 0.25`: the approved images already carry the blur of their own lens, so the full blur of the film lens on top would double it. The real backdrops get their own value in their unit.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 2 · Kept takes that sat a few pixels off their approved image were moved back before they were cut to size: frost up 4 px, golden up 2 px. Nothing else in them was changed.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 2 · The stand-in ground no longer asks for 16 tap anisotropic filtering. Measured on this renderer it cost 0.4 s per sample at preview size, three times the rest of the frame together (draft frame 0.8 s to 0.4 s, review frame 4.9 s to 1.7 s). The real ground in Phase 3 gets a one tap filter that picks its detail level from the short side of the pixel footprint and lets the samples of the frame average the long side.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 2 · `plant.heightM` is read as the height of the top of the whole plant: the arch of the hook, then the tips of the leaves, then the bracts, then the petals. The stem's own length is solved every frame so the highest point of the built plant stands at exactly that height (checked by a unit test on every seventh frame, to 0.02 mm).
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 2 · The plant is split in two files: `growth.ts` holds every stage as a function of time (hook, husk, seed leaves, leaves, bud, bracts, rays, the turn of the head) and `standin.ts` holds the stand-in shapes built from them. Phase 3 replaces the shapes and keeps the growth.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 2 · The head faces 25° to the left of the direction the companion is seen from (`field.companion.fromAzimuthDeg`) once it is open, which is what the approved hero image shows. While it opens it is turned up to 42° further toward the evening sun and comes round as the rays unfold, as the approved bloom-opening image shows.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 2 · Leaves keep their place near the head: a leaf's node rises with the plant for longer the later the leaf was born, so the last leaves stay just under the bud as the stem stretches. The stand-in has 21 bracts in three rings. Rays lie folded over the face and open by sector over about five seconds.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 2 · `performance.now` is used in the `live` and `autopilot` modes only to choose which frame to draw next. No pixel depends on it: a frame is always drawn from its frame number alone.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 2 · Camera fit. `tools/fit.ts` solves the camera at every key against measurements taken from that key's approved image: where the foot of the stem, the top of the plant or the middle of the head, the horizon and the sun land in the frame. It solves by least squares on the film's own projection and plant, a few times over because the keys that hold a backdrop also set the light, and the light leans the plant. - 2026-11-21 · Phase 2 · What the Phase 2 render budget means for the phases after it (measured 05:36 UTC on stand-ins): a frame of the bloom costs 2.95 s a sample in 1920 x 1080, so a judged still at `final` costs about 2.4 minutes and a pack of ten review stills about 10 minutes, and the whole film at `final` in 1920 x 1080 would take 47 h on one VM of this speed. `deliver.sandboxMaster` is false, so the sandbox never renders that master; it stays an export for a machine with a GPU. The real plant must not make a sample dearer than it has to: every agent that writes a shader in Phase 3 is told that the film is drawn by software WebGL, that the noise in the stand-in's shaders is the dearest thing in them, and to read maps instead. The budget is measured again in Phase 3.

- 2026-11-21 · Phase 3 · The scaffold is merged into `main` (`dfc65a0`, a merge of the local branch `p3-scaffold`). Every piece of the plant is now a part with its own folder (`src/plant/stem/`, `leaves/`, `head/bud/`, `head/disc/`, `head/petals/`, `seedling/`), each exporting `create(options)`; a part that returns nothing leaves its stand-in drawn, so the plant is whole at every commit. Hairs (`src/plant/hairs/`) and the tissue shading (`src/plant/surface/`) are shared libraries with frozen signatures. A world folder may export a `stage` (objects, a per-frame `prepare`, a sky) and the golden world is the first to be asked for one. The wind is two inputs of the pose (`wind` from 0 to 1 and the film time), brought to zero at every cut by `windEase`. Checked after the merge: tsc clean, 71 tests, build clean, stills at frames 1350 and 1515 show the stand-in plant with the example stem drawn on it, and every fact of the Phase 2 fix passes is intact (`headAzimuth`, `FACE_AZIMUTH`, `EARLY_TURN`, the neighbor sizes, the fitted keys, `SEAT`, `NECK_FLARE`, `NEIGHBOR_HEAD`, companion 37, `leftM` 0.73).
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 3 · The head's floor is one shared function, `floorAt(pose, rho, theta)` in `src/plant/skeleton.ts`: a point, its normal and the outward direction on the front of the receptacle, `rho` from 0 at the middle to 1 at the rim, `theta` from the head's `p` toward `q`. The floor stands `depth x (0.5 + 0.22 x (1 - rho x rho))` along the head's axis, a shallow dome. The receptacle, the disc florets and the feet of the rays all sit on it, so three agents working apart meet on one surface.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 3 · G8 (every generated asset has its recipe) now takes the nearest `RECIPE.md` from the asset's own folder up to its owner's folder, where it took only the owner's. A set folder (`full`, `web`) is skipped. The check is the same check: every asset still needs a recipe with its source, prompt and takes. It lets an agent keep one recipe per part folder without the Builder merging ten files into one.

- 2026-11-21 · Phase 3 · Focus: the lens now holds the visible surface of the bud and the head. `tipAt` (the growing tip) is unchanged up to the bud and from the bud on stays inside the head (when open it is the middle of the floor, where it was 1.2 cm in front of it). `focusAt` in `src/plant/standin.ts` takes the lens position and returns where the line from the lens to the tip first meets the head's ellipsoid, and both the film (`Film.poseAt`) and the state the tests read (`poseAtTime`) focus there. The tip lands on the same pixel as before, so the framing checks do not move. It is one smooth function of time: `tests/focus.test.ts` checks that the point is on the line and on the surface, on the lens side, and that the focus distance never steps more than its neighbors. Measured at the draft tier in the master format: M1 passes on frames 1350 and 1515 (before: blur circle 1.5 to 2 px at the tip on frames 1277 to 1388); the focus plane moved 5.5 cm toward the lens at 1350. The sharp zone at f/2 is about 2 cm either side at 1350, so the rim of the bud and the stem under it are soft; the f-number of the two bloom keys is set against the approved images once the real head is in.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 3 · Wind on whole plants. The stem of the plant and of the companion leans with the slowest swell of the wind alone (`sway` in `src/plant/wind.ts`, one wave about 4.4 s long at a fixed place), a steady 0.03 rad downwind and 0.05 rad of swing round it at full wind, in the same upper part of the stem that leans to the sun. The faster parts of the gust are left to the leaves and petals: a stem carrying a head is too heavy for them, and they would smear the head by 3 px in a close-up. At golden hour (wind 0.5) the head moves about 1.6 cm either way, under 1 px of motion blur in the master format. The stem is as long as it takes to stand its height in still air, so the wind bends it and never stretches it. The neighbors are one shape drawn many times, so their stems are not bent on the CPU: `plantWorld` in `src/plant/part.ts` carries every vertex of a neighbor sideways by the same bend, from the swell that reaches that neighbor's own place, so a wave crosses the field. The wind is 0 on every cut, as before. `tests/wind.test.ts` holds these rules.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 3 · The shadow map follows the lens in a close-up. Until now it always covered the whole plant (and the companion in the golden world), 3.3 m of ground on 2048 texels, 1.6 mm a texel, which is coarser than a bract's edge in the master format at 0.7 m. The square is now sized to what the lens sees at the depth it focuses on (`seen` in `Film.draw`: 1.25 times the half diagonal there, plus 2 cm) and centered on the lens axis at that depth; it goes back to the whole plant as soon as that square would be three quarters of the whole plant, and always once the lens is 1.8 m away, so `dawn`, `rain` and the pull-back keep the map they had. The near and far planes still span every caster (`reach` in `ShadowMap.render`), so a leaf outside the square still shades what is inside it. At frame 1350 the map is 1.03 m wide, 0.5 mm a texel, about three times finer. Checked by rendering frames 45, 420, 780, 1080, 1199, 1350, 1515 and 1755: no picture changed but the bloom close-ups, where the shadow edges on the bud are finer.
- 2026-11-21 · Phase 3 · The companion is drawn only when the lens can see it. `companionInView` in `src/director/companion.ts` tests a set of balls round its stem, head and leaves against the frame, generously (each ball is larger than the part it holds, plus 1.2 cm), and the film skips the companion's build, its draw and its shadow in a frame where it says no and the lens is within 1.0 m; farther back it is always drawn. It is left out of frames 0 to 612, 695 to 712 and 1017 to 1100, and never in `bloom` or `field`. Two checks that this changes no pixel. `tests/companion.test.ts` projects every vertex of the companion on every third frame of the film and fails if a frame that shows any vertex is one where it would be left out. And frames 45, 195, 300, 420, 560, 605, 700, 1040 and 1080 were rendered in the master format with the companion left out and with it forced in: all nine pairs are the same to the last bit, so its shadow reaches nothing the lens sees in those frames. For that to hold, the depth the shadow map spans is the same whether the companion is drawn or not (a first try let it shrink, and 1 to 16 values out of 6.2 million moved on four of the nine frames, at single points on a shadow edge). The companion's matrix is now one function, `companionMatrix()`, with a lean (`COMPANION_LEAN`, a slope, zero for now) that keeps its top at the height the config gives.

Costs the agents measured on a master draft still of frame 1515: bud 3.5 to 4.6 s (its 40,000 hairs, before the new hairs), rays 3.3 to 3.6 s, disc 3.5 to 3.8 s, skin and lens no change, hairs 110,000 on each plant for the price of 6,000 before. - 2026-11-21 · Phase 3 · Why M3 fails at 45 s, and that grain is not the cure. M3 is the mean edge strength of the frame inside its own sharp zone against the best region of the same size in the approved image; at 45 s ours reads 10.5 against 15.9. In smooth out of focus areas, where only grain shows, ours reads 4.8 (sky) and 4.2 (dark field) at both keys against 4.0 and 2.7 on the approved opening and 2.5 and 2.2 on the approved hero, so ours already carries as much grain as the grainier approved frame and more of it in the shadows. The gap is real detail in the sharp zone: the approved's is the stem, petioles and leaves under the head with a dense lit fringe of hairs and toothed lit margins, ours is a smooth dome of rays and a dark stem with a hard line. The lens agent is asked to make the grain follow tone and sit between the two approved frames (about 3.5 and 2.6 in those two boxes), which lowers M3 a little; the hair, stem, skin, leaf, bud and petal agents carry what has to raise it.

R4 scores 4 with one severe issue (the plant swaps shape in one frame) and two majors about the same cut (the horizon steps, the camera's speed changes). Measured: frames 1199 and 1200 on `main` show the same head and the same leaves in the same places (`out/work/scratch/view/cut-1199-1200.jpg`); G12 finds every cut's worst step at 1.00 of 1.5 for the plant's shape and the camera; the camera path gives a horizon that climbs 0.64 px a frame and a far point that pans 2.09 px a frame at 1920 wide on both sides of the cut, with no step (`out/work/scratch/cutcam.ts`). So the geometry and the move are continuous and the film's rule holds. What differs is how each world shows them: fog light shows the pale underside of the head and every leaf in grey, the golden backlight shows a dark ball and loses the right leaf against the dark field; the fog hides everything far, so the move only becomes visible after the cut; the golden world has hills above the field's edge where the fog shows only the edge. The kit lets a severe or major issue be declined only against an approved image, a choice of Brandon's or a settled line, so none of the three is declined. They are open issues of the cut with two owners: `bloom` must show the plant's form in the shade in its first frames (the skin's raised shade, less lift taken away by the lens), and in Phase 4 the `frost` unit lights the bud so that it reads closed, draws the same ridge faintly through its fog at the same height and keeps something far in view that shows the move.

- 2026-11-21 · Phase 3 · Focus shared between the head and the stem (`24522da`, 11:20 UTC; `src/director/pose.ts`, `state.ts`, `film.ts`, `src/plant/standin.ts`, `model.ts`). Why: round 3 said the stem's hairs are soft. At f/4 and 1920 wide the stem under the head lay 2.58 px of blur behind the head's held point at frame 1515 and 2.84 px at 1350, so with the focus on the held point no hair could be sharp. What: `carrierAt` gives the stem's point 0.2 m under its end; `poseOf` puts the focus between the held point and that point, evenly, and the held point never takes more than `HELD_SHARE` 0.55 of the limit `detail.focusCocPx` (0.825 px). One lens, one focus distance, one aperture for the whole frame; nothing is sharpened or blurred per object. Measured: with 0.8 M1 failed at 1350 (1.64 px, because the real surface at the held pixel lies nearer than the stand-in's point by 0.44 px there); with 0.55 M1 passes at both keys and the stem is at 1.75 px (1515) and 2.0 px (1350). f/4 stays (earlier decision: at f/5.6 the heads behind showed separate rays). So the split helps at 1350 and costs nothing at 1515; the fall at 1515 from round 3's 0.881 came from the merged passes (less sheen on the disc and the rays), not from the focus.

- 2026-11-21 · Phase 3 · The bloom clip is rendered in parts. 360 frames at 1920 x 1080 and the `review` tier take about 50 s each on this VM: five hours for 12 seconds of film, against a Phase 3 box with under three hours left. The twelve builders' VMs are the same image with the same browser, so after the last round each renders 30 frames (one second, one closed group of pictures) of the judged commit with `npm run film -- --unit bloom --first A --last B --tier review --format 16x9` and returns the chunk with its record; nothing of it is merged. The tools join them: `tools/lib/tiling.ts` picks parts that cover the unit exactly (no gap, no overlap, nothing past the unit), `joinParts` in `tools/film.ts` joins current parts into the unit's chunk and writes a record with the merged M1 result of all 360 frames and every part's machine, and `npm run clip` uses it when the whole chunk is not there. A part from another build is refused by its hashes, as any chunk is. Tests: `tests/tiling.test.ts` (6), and a dry run with twelve made-up parts, deleted afterwards. Commit `341c9d1` on the worktree's branch; it reaches `main` with the round 6 merges. The Builder checks the joined clip against its own `review` stills of frames 1350 and 1515 and looks at every seam.

- 2026-11-21 · Phase 3 · Two slips by the Builder, written down as they happened. (1) The key of round 5 went out early. `scorecards/round-005/KEY.json` was pushed at 14:16 UTC in `ff80076`, together with the jury's card, while the motion critic R4 (`reviewer_41`) had not answered. The kit says the key is pushed only after all answers. It cannot be taken back (no force push, no rebase). What bounds it: R4 is a fresh agent on its own VM that received four attachments and its rubric and nothing else, it was not told the repository or the film's name, and its part of the key lists only which of our own frames stand on each sheet; there is no approved image and no control in its pack. R1, R3 and all ten jurors had answered before the push. Rounds 3 and 4 kept the order (checked in the history: the key came in the same commit as the last card or later). Cause: the scratch helper that writes the jury's card copied the key as its last step; it now copies it only when the cards of R1, R3 and R4 exist. The cards stay word for word; the round's summary carries a note of this. (2) Thirteen agents ran for about half a minute at 14:19 UTC where `sandbox.maxAgents` allows twelve: four Phase 4 jobs were sent while `field_builder`, taken for idle after its report, was still running. `skin_builder` was interrupted at once and starts its Phase 4 job when R4's place is free. Running agents are counted before every send from now, not assumed. It happened once more at 14:22 UTC for a few seconds: the list of agents lags a send, so after `skin_builder` was woken it still showed eleven, `bud_builder`'s Phase 4 job went out, and thirteen ran; `bud_builder` was interrupted at once and waits for a place. From now a message sent counts as a running agent at once, whatever the list says.

- 2026-11-21 · Phase 3 · 14:30 to 14:50 UTC. The motion critic's three issues of round 5: causes and what was done. (1) Single frame jumps of the horizon (major). Cause found: not the camera, whose direction is a smooth function of time, but the blur. `frameSamples` turns the lens set every frame, and with the 8 samples of `review` its weighted middle sat up to a quarter of the opening's radius off the axis: in height -0.070, 0.161, -0.106 at frames 1332 to 1334 and -0.039, -0.111, 0.011 at 1339 to 1341. Everything out of focus shifts with that lean, about 10 px per unit here, which is the measured 464.9, 462.4, 465.0 and 463.7, 464.8, 463.3 px. At `final` (48 samples) the lean was under 0.02. Fix in `src/core/sampler.ts` (`727a943`): the set is moved so its weighted middle is on the axis (worst over all 1800 frames 0.0002 at 8 samples); still a pure function of the frame; the sampler's tests pass. (2) The camera between 45.4 s and 52.0 s (severe). Cause found: the keys at 45 s and 50.5 s share one view of the far background (a far point at x 1226.2 and 1226.0 px, the horizon at 507.6 and 507.5 px), but each channel ran on its own curve between them, so the background went 11.5 px right by 46.5 s and came back by 50 s, and the horizon sank 7.6 px by 48.5 s and rose again. Fix in `config/showcase.json` (`74d0051`): seven keys between the two (45.25, 45.5, 46.5, 47.5, 48.5, 49.5 and 50.25 s), solved so the view of the far background holds within 0.1 px while distance, height and azimuth run evenly and the plant slides 70 px left; the key at 52 s takes 0.6 of its step from 50.5 s, so the horizon travels 20 px instead of 33 px in the bloom's last 1.5 s while the crane's peak speed stays what it was. The fitted keys at 45 s and 50.5 s are unchanged, and every judged frame of the other units stands on a key that did not move. What the two fitted keys do not allow is a view that drifts one way through the whole bloom: it must end where it began, so the far background holds and the plant moves against it. (3) The cut at 40.00 s (severe, third round in a row). `quality.plateauRounds` is passed, so the technique changes by the ladder "A cut reads as a glitch" in `kit/04-LOOK.md`. Rung 1, the test for a cut in `kit/03-METHOD.md` (plant, grass and horizon line up): the plant and the camera line up (measured in round 4); the grass lines up (`field_builder`, 14:47 UTC: nothing in `src/field/` reads the world, places come from one fixed list, the three nearest stalks right of the plant sit at the same x in frames 1199 and 1200; what reads as other spacing is frost's fog showing only the six or so nearest silhouettes); the horizon does not: the frost stand-in ends at the bare horizon with single trees, golden draws the shared skyline about 34 px higher. `harness_builder`, the frost world's owner, was asked at 14:43 UTC to draw the shared skyline (`src/weather/skyline.ts`) through frost's fog at the same height and to drop the single trees. It goes into round 6 only if it is built, tested and pushed by 15:03 UTC; otherwise it leads the `frost` unit in Phase 4. Rungs 2 and 3 are not the trouble here: the two worlds already differ plainly in light and color, and nothing in golden starts late.

- 2026-11-21 · Phase 3 · 15:21 UTC · tool fix, `tools/film.ts`. Every one of the twelve builders reported the same thing about its clip part: the command and the file name say `review`, the part's record says `"tier": "draft"`. Cause: the film job took the record's base from the page when the session opened, before the first frame had set the tier, so the record carried the page's default; the preview films were all `draft`, so it never showed. Fixed: the record takes the tier from the job's own options. The twelve records already written were corrected by the Builder after it had checked the frames themselves (frames 1350 and 1515 out of two parts against its own review stills, 40.5 and 38.8 dB): `tier` is now `review`, the value the tool wrote is kept as `tierAsWritten`, and `tierNote` says why. No frame was touched. The tools are not part of either hash, so no still or part went stale.

- 2026-11-21 · Phase 4 · 15:40 UTC · rulings sent to the eight working builders (texts in `out/work/briefs/p4-answers/`). The shutter stays 180 degrees in the rain: `film.shutterDegrees` is one number for the film and the kit takes a streak's length from the fall speed and the shutter time, with drops spread through the depth of the scene. The approved's 27 to 75 px streaks are drops 5 to 13 m out (a 15 cm streak is about 440 px at the plant's 0.81 m), so `lens_builder` draws fine rain through that depth instead of shortening the shutter. Lightning may light the plant and ground from the rain stage's `sample` hook, which runs after `applyWorld`. The plant's own head at 1755 is 72 px wide against the approved's 96 px: the approved field image draws a plant about 0.86 of ours in height with a head 1.33 times ours, which is not the plant of the two approved bloom images; the film keeps one plant.

(2) Critics before jurors, in the order the packs are checked (frost, night, rain, dawn). (3) Four builders lend their places: `bud_builder` (idle after its report 6), and `harness_builder`, `disc_builder` and `stem_builder`, who were told at 18:20 UTC to push, answer in five lines and end their turns until the round's words reach them. (4) A round's words go to the builders as soon as its three critics have answered; the jurors' words follow when the last juror is in. Each answer is saved word for word and ticked as it comes, and the key is opened in `scorecards/` only after the last one, as before.

The rain stays at f/9: `petal_builder` measured f/11 beside it on `31344ca` with his crowns (M3 0.975 over 254270 sharp pixels against 0.937 over 215649; his picture looked at beside the approved image, no gain the Builder can see at whole-frame size), and the f-number's curve runs through every unit's keys, so f/11 at 14 s would also move the dawn between 1.5 and 6.5 s and the night and frost between 14 and 36 s (frame 60, a judged dawn frame, among them). The cause of the changed bloom frames is named and accepted. `field_builder` bisected frame 1200 at `review` against `1232b1c`: the merge before it on the line, `c760ab6` (dawn ground), gives 0 of 8294400 values; `8ae2b8f` (the leaves' posture before the bloom, `src/plant/leaves/`) gives 514810, in the box x 0 to 1919, y 534 to 1079, the same count and box as `31344ca`. `bud_builder` counted the same difference on `509fccf` further into the bloom: 219277 values at frame 1260 (largest 19), 22298 at 1320 (largest 5), 0 at 1340, byte identical. So it is the neighbors alone, whose clocks stand up to 4.6 s behind the plant and who still carry the pre-bloom posture until film time 44.6 s. No cure is taken: a neighbor that is 37 s old should carry the leaves of a 37 s old plant, and forcing the bloom's posture on it would be the fault. The bloom's kept key stills (1350, 1515) hold to the byte, which is what `kit/07-QUALITY-BAR.md` asks of a finished unit after a shared change.

## Open items

One entry per issue left open by a limit: the unit, the frame, the critic and its words, what was tried, what to try next. Parts finished with a fallback are listed here too, marked `fallback`. Issues carried from the blockout are marked with the phase that will settle them.

- Blockout · `bloom` · frames 1350 and 1515 · the foliage at the left edge. Round 1, R4 blockout: "The bloom close-up loses the large cropped left-edge foliage that frames the target". Round 2, R4 blockout, pair 6: "in 6-a a close foreground plant fills the entire left margin (y = 55 to 394) with an upper leaf crossing the sun, whereas in 6-b only a single small leaf tip enters at the bottom-left (y = 300 to 355), leaving the upper three-quarters of the left edge empty", and pair 7: "in 7-a the close left foreground plant frames the entire left edge (y = 55 to 394) across the sun, whereas in 7-b only one small leaf tip enters near the bottom-left (y = 280 to 348)". Carried to Phase 3 (the look lock on `bloom`). Tried: the companion's place and which neighbor it is, scanned twice over 90 neighbors and 52 places; larger and faster upper leaves. Its leaves reach x 0.114 and x 0.134 low in the two bloom frames; the approved images have them to about x 0.2 and up across the sun. What limits it is the rule in `kit/02-SUBJECT.md`: "Through `dawn`, `rain` and `night`, and at the `frost` key, no part of the companion is in the frame" and "keeping it out comes first". Next: the real leaves of Phase 3 are broad where the stand-in blades are narrow, and the companion's upper leaves can be given their own reach and height, which fills the edge without moving the plant; the place is scanned once more with the real leaves. If the mass still cannot be reached with the companion out of the early frames, the rest is contested under that rule and is not worked on again.
- Blockout · `bloom` · frame 1515 · the size of the heads behind the hero. Round 2, R4 blockout, pair 7: "the background blooms behind it are nearly twice as large as in 7-a (~55-68 px tall vs ~30-40 px)". Carried to Phase 3 (the look lock on `bloom`). Tried: a neighbor's head is 0.7 of the hero's (round 1) and the neighbors are shorter (round 2), so the heads stand lower and smaller behind the hero, but the nearest are still about 1.3 times the approved size. What limits it: the first row is `field.plantAheadOfRowM` (1.5 m) behind the plant and the approved image shows its nearest heads about 3 m back, while the approved `field` image shows the first row about 1 m back; no settled line covers the difference. Next: in Phase 3 the field behind the hero is a dark canopy with the lens wide open, so the heads are soft discs and their size is judged again there; if they still read too large, the nearest two rows behind the hero get heads turned further from the lens and held lower in the leaves, which is how the approved image has them.
- Blockout · `field` · frame 1755 · the hero's head and the band of field heads. Round 2, R4 blockout, pair 8: "the foreground hero plant head (at x ~ 344) is ~35% too small vertically (~31 px tall vs ~48 px in 8-a for the same ~180 px plant height) and faces flat front-on instead of three-quarters left, while the background field blooms sit entirely in a high band (y = 92 to 195) above the hero head center rather than extending down around y = 175 to 240 as in 8-a". Carried to Phase 4 (unit `field`). Fixed in the round 2 fix pass: the head is turned 33° to the left of the lens. Tried for the rest: shorter neighbors, so the first row's heads now reach down to the level of the hero's head (y 0.45 to 0.53 of the frame). Not changed: the head's width and the plant's height are fixed facts (`plant.headDiameterM`, `plant.fullHeightM`), and the approved image draws the head at about 0.28 of the plant's height where the facts give 0.16. Next: in Phase 4 the real head with its bracts and reflexed petals is measured against the approved image again, and the field's heads round the hero are judged on the real field with the grass strip in front.
- Film · M1 · 115 of 1800 frames failed in the preview film of the Phase 2 final build (`out/preview.mp4`, engine hash `883ccb0f6f431fcb`, read from its record). `night`: frames 877, 878 and 879 (blur circle at the tip 1.57, 1.73 and 1.87 px where the limit is 1.5). `bloom`: frames 1277 to 1388, all 112 of them (blur circle at the tip 1.50 to 1.99 px), which takes in the judged frame 1350. No frame holds an invalid value and the tip is inside the frame with its margin in every frame; the one reason given is the blur circle. Cause, seen in the `focus` view of frames 874, 878, 882 and 1333: the point the lens holds (`tipAt` in `src/plant/standin.ts`) lies inside the bud's body from the time the bud is large until it opens, so the focus plane cuts through the middle of the bud. Its rim is sharp and the face turned to the lens, 4 to 6 cm nearer, is outside the sharp zone. In `night` the bud is 2 cm and the same thing is at the edge of the limit for three frames. Carried to Phase 3 (the look lock on `bloom`), where M1 must be green on frames 1350 and 1515. Fixed in Phase 3 (2026-11-21 07:25 UTC): the lens holds the visible surface of the bud on the line from the lens to that point; `npm run measure -- --unit bloom --tier draft` gives M1 pass on 1350 and 1515 in the master format. Still to confirm, which keeps this item open: the film record of Phase 5 has to show no failed frame in `night` 877 to 879 and `bloom` 1277 to 1388. Round 6's build (`cd262c1`), the bloom clip at `review`, 15:21 UTC: 123 of the unit's 360 frames still fail, 1256 to 1341 (41.9 to 44.7 s, rising from 1.50 to 2.11 px), 1362 to 1383 (1.5 to 1.7 px) and 1408 to 1422 (up to 2.19 px at 1417); the judged frames pass (1.44 px at 1350). Why it is still there: since the focus is shared with the stem (`HELD_SHARE` 0.55), the held point itself takes up to 0.825 px, which leaves 0.675 px for the distance between the ellipsoid the held point lies on and the real bracts and rays in front of it, and while the bud opens that distance is larger. What to try next, in Phase 5 with the camera's last pass: read the depth the renderer drew at the tip's pixel for every frame of the unit at `draft`, and correct the held depth by a smooth curve over time so the visible surface stays under 1.5 px in every frame; measure the judged frames again after it.

## Running agents

One entry per supporting agent while it runs: its name, the folder it owns, its branch, its brief in one line, when it started, and its limit or the time of its last push.

Ten agents of the look lock, all started 2026-11-21 07:16 UTC as sandbox agents on the default model, each with the same common brief (the scaffold, the rules, the test commands, the report) and its own part brief, the two approved bloom images and photographs of its part from the reference board. Each reports once to the Builder within 100 minutes (by 08:56 UTC), pushes at least every 25 minutes, and is replaced once after 45 minutes of silence. The briefs are kept in `out/work/briefs/p3/` (not committed).
